\clearpage
\phantomsection\part*{Part VII.\quad The exact half-size crossover}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Part VII.\texorpdfstring{\quad}{ } The exact half-size crossover}

\noindent\emph{Part~VII was added on 1 October 2026 in batch~71 of
ProveIt's incoming-report intake, from one manuscript. Part~VI proved that
$\ljP(M_n\le\lfloor n/2+t\sqrt n\rfloor)\asymp n^{-1}(1+t_+)$ for bounded
$t$ (\cref{pr:cor:half}) and asked for the limiting crossover functions, the
endpoint constants and the amplitudes inside the lower phases
(\cref{pr:sec:q-amplitudes,pr:sec:q-endpoints,pr:sec:q-crossover}); Parts~IV
and~V asked for the transition function at $m=n/2$
(\cref{cjm:sec:mj-half,pr:sec:cjm-half}). Part~VII proves, locally
uniformly in real $t$,
\[
 n\,\ljP\bigl(M_n\le\lfloor n/2+t\sqrt n\rfloor\bigr)
 \longrightarrow6+\frac{12}{\sqrt\pi}\,t_+,
\]
and the equivalent $\ljP(M_n\le\lfloor\theta n\rfloor)\sim
I_3(\theta)/(2\pi n)$ throughout the first sub-half-size phase
$1/3<\theta\le1/2$, with an explicit two-dimensional integral $I_3$. This
answers Part~VI's questions on crossover functions and endpoint constants at
$q=2$ and on amplitudes at $r=2$, and the transition questions of Parts~IV
and~V. Its proofs re-derive every finite interface they use and assume no
asymptotic theorem of Parts~I--VI.}

\section{Provenance, conventions and the answer}
\label{hc:sec:intro}

\subsection{One manuscript, one addition}
\label{hc:sec:source}
Part~VII is built from one AI-assisted research manuscript,
\emph{manuscript~03} of batch~71, \emph{Exact half size crossover for
adjacency bounded $132$ avoiders}, dated 1 October 2026 (8 pages, US Letter,
as delivered; its byline reads ``AI-assisted mathematical research''). It
arrived as the archive \texttt{132\_Avoider\_Crossover\_Package.zip} (inner
directory \texttt{Half\_Size\_Crossover/}, main file
\texttt{half\_size\_crossover.tex}) in ProveIt commit \texttt{aee32ad45},
and was placed in commit \texttt{427bca743}. Its source ledger pins ProveIt
commit \texttt{29e52fcdf}, the placement commit of batch~68, at which this
report's \texttt{article.tex} had the blob \texttt{0b7cb2f2e6} and ended at
Part~V; and it read the incoming batch-68 archive
\texttt{ProveIt\_Polynomial\_Rarity.zip} (blob \texttt{cf18b47515}, arrival
commit \texttt{a866ff9a2}), whose manuscript is printed above as Part~VI.
Part~VI was written into this report after the pin, in commit
\texttt{cfdefe6e3}, whose parent still had the article blob
\texttt{0b7cb2f2e6}. The manuscript therefore cites Part~VI's results from
the incoming manuscript, which Part~VI prints in full, and its statements
about Parts~III--V refer to the text printed above (apart from the dated
batch-68 notes that Part~VI added there).

Its code, data and source ledger are shipped beside the earlier parts' files
with the prefix \path{08-half-size-crossover-}, continuing the report's local
sequence after Part~VI's \path{07-polynomial-rarity-}. Its manuscript,
README and PDF are not shipped and survive in the arrival commit; its
checksum ledger was verified at placement (11 of 11 files) and retired. Its
\path{model.py} is byte-identical to Part~VI's
\path{code/07-polynomial-rarity-model.py} (Git blob \texttt{a4fdee3761}): the
manuscript copied it from the batch-68 package and credits it as such. Both
copies are shipped, the second as
\path{code/08-half-size-crossover-model.py}, because the delivered verifier
imports it by its delivered name (\cref{hc:sec:reproduce}).

The manuscript contributes, in the order printed here: the limiting
half-size crossover (\cref{hc:thm:window}) and the first sub-half-size
equivalent (\cref{hc:thm:phase}); uniform endpoint estimates for a uniform
avoiding block (\cref{hc:lem:endpoints}); a canonical threshold parser valid
for every adjacency bound below the size (\cref{hc:lem:parser}) with its word
masses; small-word estimates (\cref{hc:lem:small}) and their use to discard
large small-word costs; the two-piece contribution, with a uniform tail at
the rare-event scale (\cref{hc:lem:pairtail}); the exact three-piece endpoint
table and its mass eight; the exclusion of every other parser family at the
scale $n^{-1}$; the evaluation $I_3(1/2)=12\pi$; finite regression checks;
an exact statement of its repository boundary; and four further questions.
Every result, proof, remark, limitation and question of the manuscript is
printed.

\begin{resultbox}
\textbf{Status (manuscript 03 of batch 71).} The theorems of Part~VII have
written mathematical proofs, which the manuscript reports were checked by
direct derivation. They are ordinary mathematical arguments, not
proof-assistant formalizations: nothing is Lean- or Rocq-verified, nothing
has been externally refereed, and no exhaustive publication priority is
claimed. Part~VII re-derives the finite interfaces it uses and does not
assume Part~IV's macroscopic equivalent \eqref{mj:eq:bulk-ratio}; its
small-word estimates are Part~VI's lemmas, re-derived. Its conclusions are
limits and asymptotic equivalents with no finite-size error rate
(\cref{hc:sec:checks}). Its finite data are regression checks, not evidence
for the limits.
\end{resultbox}

\subsection{The questions of Parts~IV--VI, and what answers them}
\label{hc:sec:answer}
With $p_n(m)=\ljP(M_n\le m)$ as in \eqref{pr:eq:prob}, Part~VII answers the
following questions in their first case.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{The crossover function at $q=2$} (Part~VI's
 \cref{pr:sec:q-crossover}). There $\mathfrak w_2(n)=\sqrt n$, and
 \cref{hc:thm:window} proves \eqref{pr:eq:Hquestion} at $q=2$ with
 \[
  \mathfrak H_2(t)=\mathfrak K_2+\mathfrak L_2t_+,\qquad
  \mathfrak K_2=6,\qquad \mathfrak L_2=\frac{12}{\sqrt\pi}.
 \]
 This is exactly Part~VI's ``particularly simple candidate'' at $q=2$, with
 the slope that matching with \eqref{mj:eq:bulk-ratio} ``would suggest''.
 The slope is proved directly, by the parser, not by that matching. For
 $q\ge3$ the question stays open (\cref{hc:sec:q-higher}).
\item \emph{The endpoint constant at $q=2$} (\cref{pr:sec:q-endpoints}).
 $\mathfrak K_2=\lim_n n\,p_n(\lfloor n/2\rfloor)$ exists and equals $6$.
 \Cref{hc:thm:window} holds along all $n$, odd and even, so the limit has no
 residue-class oscillation; $\mathfrak K_q$ for $q\ge3$ stays open.
\item \emph{The amplitude at $r=2$} (\cref{pr:sec:q-amplitudes}).
 For $1/3<\theta<1/2$, \eqref{pr:eq:amplitudequestion} holds with
 \[
  \Omega_2(\theta)=\frac{I_3(\theta)}{2\pi},
 \]
 $I_3$ as in \eqref{hc:eq:I3}, and at $\theta=1/2$ the same formula gives
 $6$. This is also the $r=2$ case of the amplitude half of Part~IV's
 \cref{mj:sec:q-hierarchy}; Part~IV's manuscript~11 asked there for an
 equivalent for every $0<\theta<1/2$, which is now known only for
 $1/3<\theta<1/2$. The amplitudes for $r\ge3$ stay open
 (\cref{hc:sec:q-lower}).
\item \emph{The transition at $m=n/2$} (Part~IV's \cref{cjm:sec:mj-half},
 Part~V's \cref{pr:sec:cjm-half}). For $s_n=t\sqrt n$, the probability scale
 is $n^{-1}$ (Part~VI) and the limiting transition function is
 $6+(12/\sqrt\pi)t_+$, continuous and piecewise linear, constant on the
 lower side.
\end{itemize}
\Cref{hc:thm:window} sharpens Part~VI's \cref{pr:cor:half} from a two-sided
order to a limit, and \cref{hc:thm:phase} sharpens \cref{pr:thm:staircase}
at $r=2$ from an order to an equivalent; nothing in Parts~I--VI is refuted.
Two consistency checks connect the new constants with the earlier parts. The
ramp slope $12/\sqrt\pi$ is the slope of Part~IV's profile at its upper end,
\eqref{mj:eq:half-edge}: substituting $\theta=1/2+t/\sqrt n$ into
\eqref{mj:eq:bulk-ratio} and multiplying by $n$ gives $12t/\sqrt\pi+o(1)$
for fixed $t>0$, although no theorem licenses that substitution. And
$I_3(\theta)\to0$ as $\theta\downarrow1/3$, because the integration domain
of \eqref{hc:eq:I3} shrinks to the single point $x=y=z=1/3$, in agreement
with the jump of the staircase exponent from $1$ to $3/2$ at $\theta=1/3$.
Part~VII does not answer Part~VI's questions on the conditioned geometry,
uniformity near reciprocal boundaries, growing reciprocal index, effective
bounds, other classes, other tail indices or formalization
(\cref{pr:sec:q-geometry,pr:sec:q-uniform,pr:sec:q-growing,pr:sec:q-effective,pr:sec:q-classes,pr:sec:q-tails,pr:sec:q-formal}).
[Added 1 October 2026, batch 72: Part~VIII answers the cases $q\ge3$ and
$r\ge3$ that the list above leaves open, and Part~VI's question on the
conditioned geometry at the level of the parser's macroscopic pieces
(\cref{gc:sec:answer}).]

\section{Results and research boundary}
\label{hc:sec:results}
A permutation $\pi\in S_n$ avoids $132$ if no $i<j<k$ satisfy
$\pi_i<\pi_k<\pi_j$. The set $\Av_n(132)$ has Catalan cardinality
$C_n=(n+1)^{-1}\binom{2n}{n}$. Put
\[
 M(\pi)=\max_{1\le i<n}|\pi_{i+1}-\pi_i|,
 \qquad p_n(m)=\frac{\#\{\pi\in\Av_n(132):M(\pi)\le m\}}{C_n}.
\]
An empty maximum is zero. (These are Part~VI's \eqref{pr:eq:objects} and
\eqref{pr:eq:prob}.) Part~VI, from the manuscript that this one inspected
as an incoming archive (\cref{hc:sec:source}), proves
\[
 p_n(m)\asymp n^{-1}+n^{-3/2}\prpos{2m-n}
\]
uniformly near $m/n=1/2$ (\cref{pr:cor:half}), but explicitly leaves
limiting crossover functions and exact amplitudes open
(\cref{pr:sec:q-amplitudes,pr:sec:q-crossover}). The following strengthens
that comparison to a limit.

\begin{theorem}[Half-size crossover]\label{hc:thm:window}
For every $B<\infty$,
\[
 \sup_{|t|\le B}\left|
 n p_n\!\left(\left\lfloor n/2+t\sqrt n\right\rfloor\right)
 -6-\frac{12}{\sqrt\pi}\prpos t\right|\longrightarrow0.
\]
In particular, $p_n(\lfloor n/2\rfloor)\sim6/n$.
\end{theorem}

Define
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:I3}
 I_3(\theta)=\iint_{\substack{x+y+z=1\\0<x,y,z\le\theta}}
 (xyz)^{-3/2}\,\dd x\,\dd y,\qquad z=1-x-y.
\end{equation}
The measure is the coordinate measure $\dd x\,\dd y$, not Euclidean surface
area on the plane.
\begin{theorem}[First sub-half-size equivalent]\label{hc:thm:phase}
For $1/3<\theta\le1/2$,
\[
 p_n(\lfloor\theta n\rfloor)\sim\frac{I_3(\theta)}{2\pi n}.
\]
The convergence is uniform on compact subintervals of $(1/3,1/2)$. The
integral is finite and positive, and $I_3(1/2)=12\pi$.
\end{theorem}

The fixed-bound enumeration was initiated by Nadler~\cite{Nadler} and
developed by Mayama and Akita~\cite{MayamaAkita}. The present argument uses
elementary Catalan interfaces appearing in Parts~III--IV of this report
(\cref{mj:sec:first,mj:sec:last,mj:sec:parser}), and a small-letter
estimate appearing in Part~VI (\cref{pr:lem:local,pr:lem:small}). We
rederive the needed estimates. We do not infer any infinite claim from
numerical data, and do not assume the earlier macroscopic equivalent. Related
fewest-big-jumps results, such as~\cite{pr:KerriouMorters}, provide context
but are not invoked as theorems here.

\section{Finite Catalan interfaces}
\label{hc:sec:interfaces}
Write $c_j=4^{-j}C_j$. Stirling's formula and elementary bounds give
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:cat}
 c_j\sim\frac1{\sqrt\pi j^{3/2}},\qquad
 c_-(j+1)^{-3/2}\le c_j\le c_+(j+1)^{-3/2}.
\end{equation}
(This is Part~IV's \eqref{mj:eq:catalan-bounds}.) The maximum
decomposition is unique:
\[
 \pi=(\sigma+b)\,N\,\tau,\quad
 \sigma\in\Av_a(132),\quad\tau\in\Av_b(132),\quad a+b+1=N.
\]
Every left entry exceeds every right entry. Conversely, arbitrary avoiding
children with these value sets give an avoider. If $b>0$ and $M(\pi)\le m$,
then $a+1\le m$, because the jump following $N$ is at least $a+1$. The left
internal jumps and the jump into $N$ are smaller than this downward boundary
jump.

For a block of size $N$ let $F,L$ be its first and last entries, and $J=N-F$
its first deficiency.
\begin{lemma}[Endpoint estimates]\label{hc:lem:endpoints}
For an unrestricted uniform avoiding block,
\begin{align}
 \ljP(J\ge h)&\le(h+2)2^{-h},\label{hc:eq:J}\\
 \#\{\pi\in\Av_N(132):L(\pi)=\ell\}&=C_{N-\ell}C_{\ell-1}.
 \label{hc:eq:L}
\end{align}
For $1\le r\le N$, uniformly,
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:halfmass}
 \ljP(L\ge r)=\frac12+
 O\!\left(r^{-1/2}+(N-r+1)^{-1/2}\right).
\end{equation}
In particular $L/N\Rightarrow\tfrac12\delta_0+\tfrac12\delta_1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The first-entry count at $F=N-j$ is
\[
 \frac{j+1}{N}\binom{2N-j-2}{N-1}.
\]
This is the Catalan triangle: it follows, for example, from the first-entry
generating function $(x\Cat(x))^{j+1}$ and Lagrange inversion applied to
$\Cat=1+x\Cat^2$. Division by $C_N$ gives
\[
 (j+1)\frac{N+1}{2(2N-1)}
 \prod_{u=0}^{j-1}\frac{N-1-u}{2N-2-u}
 \le(j+1)2^{-j-1}
\]
for $N\ge2$; summation proves \eqref{hc:eq:J}, and $N=1$ is immediate.

For the last entry $\ell$, the entries larger than $\ell$ must precede all
entries smaller than $\ell$: otherwise a smaller entry, a larger entry and
the final $\ell$ form a $132$. Thus $\pi=(\alpha+\ell)\beta\ell$, with
independent avoiding blocks of sizes $N-\ell$ and $\ell-1$. This proves
\eqref{hc:eq:L}. The distribution is symmetric under $\ell\mapsto N+1-\ell$.
The Catalan bounds give $\ljP(L=\ell)\le K\ell^{-3/2}$ for $\ell\le N/2$, and
the reflected bound at the other endpoint. Summing from an endpoint cutoff
to the middle yields \eqref{hc:eq:halfmass}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Merge note: the endpoint estimates in Parts~III, IV and~VI]
The first-entry count is Part~IV's \eqref{mj:eq:first-count}
(Part~III's \eqref{lj:eq:first-count}, Part~VI's \eqref{pr:eq:firstentry}),
and \eqref{hc:eq:J} with its proof is Part~IV's \cref{mj:lem:first},
\eqref{mj:eq:first-tail}, the same computation. Equation~\eqref{hc:eq:L} is Part~IV's
\eqref{mj:eq:last-count} with the same decomposition. Equation~\eqref{hc:eq:halfmass}
is uniform in $1\le r\le N$, with an explicit error at both ends; Part~IV's
\eqref{mj:eq:last-half} states the same for $r$ in $[\eta N,(1-\eta)N]$
only, by the same symmetry argument, and its limit statement is
\eqref{mj:eq:last-limit}. The manuscript's proofs are kept because they are
short and self-contained.
\end{remark}

\section{A parser that remains valid below half size}
\label{hc:sec:parser}
Let $R_\beta$ attach the left decoration $\beta$ and the new maximum before a
current avoiding block. Its cost is $k=|\beta|+1$. Let $A$ append a new
maximum; its cost is one. Each operation preserves avoidance. If the old last
deficiency is $Q$, then $R_\beta$ changes it to $Q+k$. An append is accepted
precisely when $Q+1\le m$ and then sets last deficiency to zero.

\begin{lemma}[Canonical threshold parsing]\label{hc:lem:parser}
Every $m$-bounded avoider of size $n>m$ has a unique representation by a
decorated outer word in $R,A$ applied to a terminal core
\[
 (\sigma+b)N\tau,\qquad a,b\le m,
 \qquad N=a+b+1>m.
\]
Every outer $R$ has cost at most $m$. Conversely, reconstruction from such a
core and word is injective, and every accepted reconstruction has the
prescribed parser.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
At each maximum decomposition, follow a child of size $>m$ if there is one.
With a nonempty right sibling, the left size plus one is at most $m$, so a
large child is unique. A large right child records $R$; a large left child
necessarily has empty right sibling and records $A$. Stop when both children
have size at most $m$. Sizes decrease strictly, and the size on stopping
exceeds $m$. Reconstruction reverses these unique maximum decompositions. At
every intermediate step the followed core exceeds $m$, while an $R$
decoration has size below $m$, so the reconstructed parser follows exactly
the specified core.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Merge note: Part~IV's parser]
\Cref{hc:lem:parser} is Part~IV's \cref{mj:lem:parse} (manuscript~11 of
batch~52), with the same parsing rule, extended from the range
$n/2\le m\le n-1$ of \eqref{mj:eq:threshold-range} to every $m<n$. Above
half size two children larger than $m$ are excluded by their total size;
below it, as the proof above shows, they are excluded by the positional
restriction alone, so uniqueness survives. The price is that the terminal
core no longer has fixed cost: its children may be small, and the outer word
may contain macroscopic $R$ letters, which \cref{hc:sec:remainders}
controls.
\end{remark}

The total normalized weight of a finite decorated word is obtained by giving
a word of cost $s$ weight $4^{-s}$. Write $\mathcal W_R$ for words with no
$A$, and $\mathcal W_A=\mathcal W-\mathcal W_R$ for words with at least one
$A$. The Catalan identities give
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:masses}
 \mathcal W(1/4)=\left.\frac1{1-z\Cat(z)-z}\right|_{z=1/4}=4,
 \qquad \mathcal W_R(1/4)=\left.\frac1{1-z\Cat(z)}\right|_{z=1/4}=2,
 \qquad \mathcal W_A(1/4)=2.
\end{equation}
The unnormalized cost generating function is $\Cat(z)^2$, whose cost-$s$
coefficient is $C_{s+1}$. In particular the normalized cost tail is
$O((s+1)^{-3/2})$ locally. (These are Part~IV's
\eqref{mj:eq:W}--\eqref{mj:eq:WR} at $z=1/4$ and
\eqref{mj:eq:word-counts}.)

\section{Uniform bounds for small words}
\label{hc:sec:smallwords}
Fix $\varepsilon>0$. An outer $R$ is large if its cost exceeds
$\varepsilon n$. All remaining letters form small words, with normalized
series
\[
 S_n(z)=\frac z4+\sum_{k\le\lfloor\varepsilon n\rfloor}
 C_{k-1}4^{-k}z^k,\qquad T_n(z)=(1-S_n(z))^{-1}.
\]
For sufficiently large $n$, the first term accounts for $A$ and the sum for
all small $R$ operations; in particular cost one has two letters. Since
$S_n(1)\le3/4$, these are subcritical series. (They are Part~VI's
\eqref{pr:eq:smallkernel} for the upper kernel \eqref{pr:eq:weightsplus},
whose mass is $\bar w_+=3/4$.)

\begin{lemma}[Small-word estimates]\label{hc:lem:small}
For every fixed positive integer $h$, uniformly in $n$ and $\varepsilon$,
\begin{align}
 T_n(1)^h&\le4^h,\label{hc:eq:massbound}\\
 [z^s]T_n(z)^h&\le K_h(s+1)^{-3/2},\label{hc:eq:local}\\
 \sum_s s[z^s]T_n(z)^h&\le K_h\sqrt{\varepsilon n}
 \quad(\varepsilon n\ge1).\label{hc:eq:moment}
\end{align}
Given $c,A_0>0$, choosing $\varepsilon$ sufficiently small gives
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:suppress}
 [z^s]T_n(z)^h=O_h(n^{-A_0})\qquad(s\ge cn),
\end{equation}
with the same choice of $\varepsilon$ for any prescribed finite set of $h$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The mass bound is immediate. In a composition of $s$ with $l$ letters, mark
a largest letter, whose size is at least $s/l$. Its weight is at most
$Kl^{3/2}s^{-3/2}$. There are at most $l$ choices for the mark, and the other
letters have total weight at most $(3/4)^{l-1}$. Expanding
$T_n^h=\sum_{l\ge0}\binom{l+h-1}{h-1}S_n^l$ proves \eqref{hc:eq:local} by a
convergent polynomial-geometric sum. Differentiate $T_n^h$ at one and use
$S_n'(1)=O(\sqrt{\varepsilon n})$ to obtain \eqref{hc:eq:moment}.

For \eqref{hc:eq:suppress}, choose $L>A_0/c$ and then $L\varepsilon<1/2$.
With $\lambda=L\log n/n$,
\[
 S_n(e^\lambda)-S_n(1)
 \le\lambda e^{\lambda\varepsilon n}
 \sum_{k\le\varepsilon n}k[z^k]S_n
 =O\!\left((\log n)n^{L\varepsilon-1/2}\right)=o(1).
\]
Thus $T_n(e^\lambda)$ stays bounded. Positivity bounds the coefficient at
$s\ge cn$ by $e^{-\lambda s}T_n(e^\lambda)^h=O_h(n^{-A_0})$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Merge note: Part~VI's lemmas]
Equations~\eqref{hc:eq:local} and \eqref{hc:eq:suppress} are Part~VI's
\cref{pr:lem:local} and \cref{pr:lem:small} for the upper kernel, with tail
index $\alpha=1/2$; the proofs are the same (Part~VI's condition
$L\varepsilon<\alpha$ is $L\varepsilon<1/2$ here). Equation~\eqref{hc:eq:moment} is a
first-moment bound for the truncated series, which grows like
$\sqrt{\varepsilon n}$; Part~VI's \eqref{pr:eq:momentbound} bounds the
moments of order $p>\alpha$ of a partial sum instead. The manuscript credits
the local bound and the tilt to Part~VI's manuscript and re-derives them.
\end{remark}

Choose $\varepsilon<1/16$ small enough that \eqref{hc:eq:suppress} holds
with $c=1/4$, $A_0=10$. A parser word has at most $\lceil1/\varepsilon\rceil$
large letters. If it has $j$ large letters, its $j+1$ intervening gaps are
counted by $T_n^{j+1}$. For this fixed $\varepsilon$, large-gap estimates may
depend on $j$ and $\varepsilon$, but the polynomial exponent $A_0$ need not
grow with $j$: after applying \eqref{hc:eq:suppress}, sum every remaining
normalized letter and core weight without a size constraint. Their total is
bounded by $4(1/2)^j$. Division by $c_n$ loses only $O(n^{3/2})$. Hence total
small-gap cost at least $n/4$ contributes $o(n^{-1})$ in every case.

\begin{remark}[Editorial note, 1 October 2026: the bound $4(1/2)^j$]
\label{hc:rem:jbound}
The preceding paragraph does not say where the factor $(1/2)^j$ comes from,
and one reading of ``every remaining normalized letter'' (all letters,
including those of the gaps) makes it false. Here is a justification, added
in the write phase and checked for this report. Fix $j\le\lceil1/\varepsilon\rceil$
and a total small-gap cost $s\ge n/4$. A parser word with $j$ large letters
splits uniquely as $g_0R_{\beta_1}g_1\cdots R_{\beta_j}g_j$ with small gaps
$g_i$, and its terminal core is $(\sigma+b)N\tau$. Dropping every adjacency
constraint only increases the count, and the total size is the sum of the
costs and of the core size, so the normalized weight of all such
configurations is at most the product of three factors:
\begin{itemize}
\item the gaps: $[z^s]T_n(z)^{j+1}$, which is $O_j(n^{-A_0})$ by
 \eqref{hc:eq:suppress} with $h=j+1$;
\item the large letters: each carries at most
 $\sum_{k\ge1}C_{k-1}4^{-k}=\tfrac14\Cat(1/4)=\tfrac12$, the ``critical total
 weight of one $R$ operation'' of Part~IV's proof of
 \cref{mj:cor:wordstats}, so together at most $(1/2)^j$;
\item the core: at most $\sum_{a,b\ge0}C_aC_b4^{-(a+b+1)}=\tfrac14\Cat(1/4)^2=1$.
\end{itemize}
Summing over the at most $n+1$ values of $s$ gives the normalized weight
$O_j(n^{1-A_0})(1/2)^j$; the paragraph's ``$4(1/2)^j$'' is this bound for the
letters and the core, with the core's mass $1$ enlarged to $4$, and the gap
factor kept from \eqref{hc:eq:suppress}. Division by $c_n$ gives
$O_j(n^{5/2-A_0})=O_j(n^{-15/2})$, and the finitely many values of $j$ give
$o(n^{-1})$, as stated. The dependence on $j$ is harmless: the proof of
\eqref{hc:eq:suppress} chooses $\varepsilon$ from $c$ and $A_0$ alone, and
$h=j+1$ enters only through $T_n(e^\lambda)^h\le5^h$ for large $n$. The
tempting false reading sums the gap letters without a size constraint as
well; that gives $T_n(1)^{j+1}(1/2)^j\le4^{j+1}2^{-j}=4\cdot2^j$, not
$4(1/2)^j$, and loses the suppression, so it cannot be the intended step.
The conclusion of the paragraph stands. (The three masses $1/2$, $1$ and
$S_n(1)\le3/4$ were recomputed exactly for this note.)
\end{remark}

\section{The two-piece contribution in the window}
\label{hc:sec:twopiece}
Set
\[
 m=\lfloor n/2+t\sqrt n\rfloor,\quad
 \Delta=2m-n,\quad |t|\le B.
\]
First consider parser words with no large $R$. If their cost is $s\le n/4$,
the terminal children are both at least $n/4-O(\sqrt n)$. Their weight
divided by $C_n$ is $O(4^{-s}n^{-3/2})$, and there are at most
$(\Delta+s+2)_+$ possible splits.

\begin{lemma}[Uniform tail at the rare-event scale]\label{hc:lem:pairtail}
For any cost cutoff $H$, the no-large-$R$ contribution from $s>H$, multiplied
by $n$, is at most
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:pairtail}
 K_B\left((H+1)^{-1/2}+\sqrt\varepsilon\right)+o(1).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Costs $s>n/4$ were suppressed above. For the rest, apply
\cref{hc:lem:small} to
\[
 K n^{-1/2}\sum_{s>H}(\Delta_++s+2)[z^s]T_n.
\]
The constant term is bounded by $K_B\sum_{s>H}(s+1)^{-3/2}$, and the
first-moment term by $K\sqrt\varepsilon$.
\end{proof}

For a fixed decorated word $w$ of cost $s$, define
\[
 \chi(w)=\begin{cases}1,&w\text{ has no }A,\\1/2,&w\text{ has an }A.\end{cases}
\]
(This is Part~IV's acceptance weight \eqref{mj:eq:acceptance}.) If $t<0$,
there are no terminal splits for all large $n$; if $t=0$, there are only
$O(1)$, giving $o(n^{-1})$. If $t>0$, their number is $2t\sqrt n+O(1)$.
Delete $h_n$ splits near each end, with $h_n\to\infty$ and
$h_n=o(\sqrt n)$. On the remaining splits, the slack below the adjacency cap
grows, so \eqref{hc:eq:J} makes the downward constraints asymptotically
automatic. If $w$ contains an append, its first such append is accepted with
probability $1/2+o(1)$ by \eqref{hc:eq:halfmass}; subsequent appends have
bounded jumps after the endpoint reset. No endpoint independence is needed:
removing a first-deficiency event of probability $o(1)$ changes every other
acceptance probability by at most $o(1)$.

Uniform Stirling at these near-half splits gives
\[
 \frac{C_aC_b}{C_n}
 \sim\frac{8\,4^{-s-1}}{\sqrt\pi n^{3/2}}.
\]
Therefore the contribution of $w$, multiplied by $n$, tends to
$4t_+\chi(w)4^{-s}/\sqrt\pi$. Summing costs up to $H$, then using
\cref{hc:lem:pairtail}, gives in the order $n\to\infty$, $H\to\infty$,
$\varepsilon\downarrow0$ the limit
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:ramp}
 \frac{4t_+}{\sqrt\pi}\sum_w\chi(w)4^{-\ljcost(w)}
 =\frac{4t_+}{\sqrt\pi}\left(2+\frac12\cdot2\right)
 =\frac{12t_+}{\sqrt\pi}.
\end{equation}

\begin{remark}[Merge note: the two-piece family is Part~IV's]
The sum in \eqref{hc:eq:ramp} is Part~IV's word mass \eqref{mj:eq:wordmass},
$3$, and the normalized weights $\chi(w)4^{-\ljcost(w)}/3$ are Part~IV's
limiting outer-word law \eqref{mj:eq:word-law}, whose cost marginal is
$\ljP(T_W=t)=(C_t+C_{t+1})/(6\,4^t)$ (\eqref{mj:eq:cost-law}). The argument
above is Part~IV's fixed-word computation (\cref{mj:prop:fixedword}), moved
from a fixed split fraction $u<1/2$ to the moving window
$|a/n-1/2|\le t/\sqrt n$, where the split kernel $\mathfrak k(y)$ of
\eqref{mj:eq:kernel} is $\mathfrak k(1/2)=8$ and the window has width
$2t/\sqrt n$; that product, $16t/\sqrt n$, times $3/(4\sqrt\pi)$ is the ramp
$12t/(\sqrt\pi\sqrt n)$ on Part~IV's scale (the merge's consistency check, not a proof). New are the uniform tail
\cref{hc:lem:pairtail} at the rarer scale $n^{-1}$ and the deletion of the
large-$R$ families, which do not exist above half size.
\end{remark}

\section{The three-piece contribution}
\label{hc:sec:threepiece}
Now require exactly one large $R$ and both terminal children larger than
$\varepsilon n$. Write the word as $w_1R_\beta w_2$, with $w_1,w_2$ the two
small-word gaps. For fixed gaps of combined cost $s$, set
\[
 k=|\beta|+1,\quad x=k/n,\quad y=a/n,\quad z=b/n.
\]
The limiting domain is $x+y+z=1$, $0<x,y,z\le1/2$. Restrict initially to a
compact subset of its interior. The first deficiencies in $\beta,\sigma,\tau$
are uniformly tight by \cref{hc:lem:endpoints}, and all costs have linear
slack below $m$, so every $R$ join is accepted apart from a vanishing
fraction.

The only remaining conditions come from appends. If $\tau$ has its low
last-endpoint mode, the terminal last deficiency is asymptotic to
$(y+z)n>m$; neither gap may contain an append. If it has its high mode, the
terminal deficiency is asymptotic to $yn<m$. An append in $w_2$ resets it,
so after the large $R$ the deficiency is asymptotic to $xn<m$ and $w_1$ is
arbitrary. If $w_2$ has no append, the large $R$ instead makes the
deficiency asymptotic to $(x+y)n>m$, so $w_1$ must have no append. Each
endpoint mode has limiting probability one half. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:chi2}
 \chi(w_1,w_2)=
 \begin{cases}
 1,&w_1,w_2\text{ both have no }A,\\
 1/2,&w_2\text{ has }A\text{ and }w_1\text{ is arbitrary},\\
 0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
Its summed normalized mass is
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:8}
 \mathcal W_R(1/4)^2+\tfrac12\mathcal W(1/4)\mathcal W_A(1/4)=4+4=8.
\end{equation}

There are three Catalan factors, with two maximum vertices outside them.
Uniformly on the compact interior,
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:tripleweight}
 \frac{C_{k-1}C_aC_b}{C_n}
 \sim\frac{4^{-s-2}}{\pi n^3}(xyz)^{-3/2}.
\end{equation}
The two free size coordinates give a Riemann sum with $n^2$ cells.
Equations~\eqref{hc:eq:chi2}--\eqref{hc:eq:tripleweight} therefore give a
limiting contribution $I_{3,\varepsilon}(1/2)/(2\pi)$ after multiplication by
$n$, where the integral initially restricts all three coordinates to be at
least $\varepsilon$. Removing this final cutoff gives $I_3(1/2)/(2\pi)$.

Here is the precise justification for removing them. For fixed
$\varepsilon$, the unrestricted weight of this family at any fixed total gap
cost, divided by $c_n$, is $O_\varepsilon(n^{-1})$: every macroscopic
variable is at least $\varepsilon n$, there are $O(n^2)$ choices, and the
three factors contribute $O_\varepsilon(n^{-9/2})$. By \eqref{hc:eq:local},
removing gap costs above $H$ costs $O_\varepsilon(H^{-1/2})$ after scaling by
$n$. For fixed $H$, remove a strip of width $\eta$ at every cap boundary; its
integral and Riemann sum are $O_\varepsilon(\eta)$. On the remaining compact
set all endpoint estimates are uniform. Take $n\to\infty$, then
$\eta\downarrow0$, then $H\to\infty$. The bounds in the next section permit
$\varepsilon\downarrow0$.

\section{Excluding all other parser families}\label{hc:sec:remainders}
The stopping condition, rather than an unrestricted renewal majorant, is
essential for small terminal children. If one terminal child has size
$a\le\varepsilon n$, its sibling obeys
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:capinterval}
 m-a\le b\le m,
\end{equation}
so there are at most $a+1$ choices for $b$. This includes $a=0$ and, after
exchanging names, $b=0$.

For exactly one large $R$ and small-gap cost $s\le n/4$, both its cost $k$
and the large terminal child $b$ are bounded below by a positive multiple of
$n$. Their normalized product is $O(n^{-3})$. Once $a,b,s$ are fixed, $k$ is
determined. Summing \eqref{hc:eq:capinterval}, either choice of small child,
and the bounded gap mass gives
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:corner}
 \ljP(\text{one large }R,\ \min(a,b)\le\varepsilon n)
 \le K n^{-3/2}\sum_{a\le\varepsilon n}(a+1)c_a+o(n^{-1})
 \le K\sqrt\varepsilon\,n^{-1}+o(n^{-1}).
\end{equation}
The large-gap part is suppressed by \eqref{hc:eq:suppress}. If there is no
large $R$ and a terminal child is small, the gap cost is at least
$n-m-\varepsilon n-1>n/4$, so it is suppressed as well.

Suppose there are $j\ge2$ large outer $R$ operations. If both terminal
children exceed $\varepsilon n$, there are at most $O(n^{j+1})$ choices at a
fixed gap cost, and $j+2$ macroscopic Catalan factors. Division by $c_n$
gives
\begin{equation}\label{hc:eq:many}
 O_\varepsilon\!\left(n^{j+1}n^{-3(j+2)/2}n^{3/2}\right)
 =O_\varepsilon\!\left(n^{-(j+1)/2}\right)=O_\varepsilon(n^{-3/2}).
\end{equation}
If one terminal child is small, \eqref{hc:eq:capinterval} leaves only
$O(n^{j-1}(a+1))$ choices for the $j+1$ large variables. Their weight is
$O_\varepsilon(n^{-3(j+1)/2})$; summing $(a+1)c_a$ costs
$O(\sqrt{\varepsilon n})$. Division by $c_n$ gives the same order as
\eqref{hc:eq:many}. Gap masses are bounded by \eqref{hc:eq:massbound}; the
number of possible $j$ is finite at fixed $\varepsilon$. All these families
are $o(n^{-1})$.

These estimates exhaust the parser. They also directly explain the
integrability at $\theta=1/2$: when one coordinate $x$ approaches zero, the
allowed interval for a second coordinate has width $x$, giving local mass
$O(x^{-1/2}\,\dd x)$ and therefore an $O(\sqrt\varepsilon)$ omitted corner.

\section{Evaluation and completion of the proofs}
\label{hc:sec:evaluation}
Integrating the $y$ coordinate first gives
\begin{align*}
 I_3(1/2)
 &=8\int_0^{1/2}
   x^{-1/2}(1-x)^{-2}(1-2x)^{-1/2}\,\dd x\\
 &=8\sqrt2\int_0^{\pi/2}
   \left(1-\tfrac12\sin^2\phi\right)^{-2}\,\dd\phi
 =12\pi.
\end{align*}
The substitution in the second line is $x=\tfrac12\sin^2\phi$. For the final
evaluation, differentiating
$\int_0^{\pi/2}(1-a\sin^2\phi)^{-1}\,\dd\phi=\pi/(2\sqrt{1-a})$ gives
\[
 \int_0^{\pi/2}(1-a\sin^2\phi)^{-2}\,\dd\phi
 =\frac{\pi(2-a)}{4(1-a)^{3/2}}.
\]
The three-piece contribution is thus $12\pi/(2\pi)=6$. Add \eqref{hc:eq:ramp};
the remainder estimates prove \cref{hc:thm:window} pointwise. For every $n$,
the scaled probability is nondecreasing in $t$, and its limit is continuous.
A finite partition of any compact interval upgrades pointwise convergence to
uniform convergence.

For fixed $1/3<\theta<1/2$, no-large-$R$ words require a positive linear cost
and are suppressed by the same tilt with $c<1-2\theta$. With one large $R$,
the domain is that of \eqref{hc:eq:I3}; every coordinate is at least
$1-2\theta>0$. Every pair of coordinates is at least $1-\theta>\theta$, so
the endpoint table \eqref{hc:eq:chi2} remains unchanged. The same Riemann sum
gives \cref{hc:thm:phase}; all remainders are uniform on compact phase
interiors. Its endpoint $\theta=1/2$ follows from \cref{hc:thm:window}.

\section{Checks and provenance}
\label{hc:sec:checks}

\subsection{Finite regression checks}
\label{hc:sec:finite}
The package includes an exact endpoint recurrence and a script comparing it
to independent generation of every $132$-avoider through size eight. The
table records finite values at the central threshold. These are
reproducible regression checks, not proof of a limit.
\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tabular}{rr}
\toprule
$n$ & $n p_n(\lfloor n/2\rfloor)$\\
\midrule
64&4.629846221170498\\
96&4.868996036164201\\
128&5.016273257983964\\
192&5.194724686827719\\
256&5.302598836522250\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{The scaled central probability, from exact counts; the limit is
$\mathfrak K_2=6$ (\cref{hc:thm:window}). The rows $n\le128$ are those of
Part~VII's shipped exact-count CSV file (\cref{hc:sec:reproduce}) at $t=0$
(and of Part~VI's \cref{pr:tab:numerics}, $\theta=1/2$); the rows $192$ and
$256$ come from the delivered verifier's longer run, whose output is not
shipped.}\label{hc:tab:finite}
\end{table}
Convergence can be slow, particularly on the negative side of the window,
where the limiting constant arises from an integrable corner. No finite-size
error rate is asserted here.

[Merge note. The ``independent generation'' is the generator
\texttt{avoiders} of the credited model module, which builds the avoiders of
each size by the maximum decomposition; the delivered verifier compares the
endpoint counter with it for all $n\le8$, $1\le m\le n$ (36 counts) and
checks the parser of \cref{hc:lem:parser} by reconstruction on every bounded
avoider there (5,599 cases), but it does not test the generator against the
$132$ predicate. Part~VI's verifier did, through $n=7$, for the same
module.]

\subsection{Rebuilding and rerunning}
\label{hc:sec:reproduce}
Part~VII's files are
\begin{lstlisting}
code/08-half-size-crossover-model.py    The credited endpoint model
                                        (byte-identical to Part VI's
                                        code/07-polynomial-rarity-model.py)
code/08-half-size-crossover-verify.py   Generator, parser and word checks;
                                        exact-count table (standard library)
code/08-half-size-crossover-build.sh    The manuscript's three-pass build
data/08-half-size-crossover-*           The exact-count CSV, the recorded
                                        run (JSON, and its stdout capture)
                                        and the delivery validation record
08-half-size-crossover-SOURCES.md       Pinned sources, literature ledger
                                        and novelty boundary
\end{lstlisting}
The programs are shipped byte-identical to the delivery and still use the
delivered names: \path{verify.py} imports \texttt{model}, which in this
directory would load Part~I's different \path{code/model.py} (it has no
\texttt{EndpointCounter}), and it writes \path{finite_checks.csv} and
\path{verification.json} beside itself; \path{build.sh} builds a
\path{half_size_crossover.tex} beside itself, which is not shipped (its text
is printed here). The report's README gives a recipe that restores the
delivered layout in a scratch copy; there, the manuscript's instructions
apply,
\begin{verbatim}
python verify.py
python verify.py --max-n 256
\end{verbatim}
and rerunning overwrites only the copy's recorded outputs. The checks require
only Python's standard library; the longer run takes several minutes.

true at the pin. Part~VI has since been printed above from that same archive
(\cref{pr:sec:source}), so in Part~VII every reference to the incoming
Polynomial Rarity manuscript is printed as a reference to Part~VI; its
order-level half-size result is \cref{pr:cor:half}, its local bound
\cref{pr:lem:local} and its small-letter suppression \cref{pr:lem:small}.]

The inherited ingredients are Catalan maximum decomposition, endpoint counts,
threshold parsing and word masses, plus the subcritical local bound and
small-letter exponential tilt. The new contribution is the three-piece
endpoint table, its exact mass eight, the corner exclusions at scale
$n^{-1}$, the leading integral and the limiting ramp. The proof has been
checked by direct derivation. It has not been externally refereed or
machine-formalized.

The primary arXiv records~\cite{Nadler,MayamaAkita,pr:KerriouMorters} and
targeted searches were checked on 1 October 2026. No matching exact
half-window result was located in that limited search. This statement does
not establish exhaustive priority. No third-party paper is redistributed in
the package.

\subsection{Where the merge had to choose}
\label{hc:sec:merge}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Placement.} Part~VII follows Part~VI and precedes Part~I's
 appendices, which contain no numbered equations, tables or figures; no
 number of Parts~I--VI changed. The manuscript's title page and abstract
 are replaced by the opening note of Part~VII, the status box of
 \cref{hc:sec:source} and a paragraph of the report's abstract; every claim
 of its abstract is stated in \cref{hc:sec:results,hc:sec:source}. Its
 Sections~1--8 are \cref{hc:sec:results,hc:sec:interfaces,hc:sec:parser,hc:sec:smallwords,hc:sec:twopiece,hc:sec:threepiece,hc:sec:remainders,hc:sec:evaluation};
 its Section~9 is this section, without its ``Further questions'', which
 open \cref{hc:sec:research}. Its unnumbered table of finite values is
 \cref{hc:tab:finite}, with a caption that is the merge's. The subsection
 on rebuilding and rerunning is the merge's. The manuscript's metadata
 author line reads ``AI assisted mathematical research''; it names no
 addressee.
\item \emph{Citations.} The manuscript cites its two repository inputs as
 bibliography entries: the report at the pin (``Parts III--V'') and the
 incoming Polynomial Rarity archive. Both are replaced by references to
 Parts~IV and~VI by label (\cref{hc:sec:results,hc:sec:boundary}); its
 Kerriou--M\"orters entry is Part~VI's \cite{pr:KerriouMorters}, and its
 Nadler and Mayama--Akita entries are the report's. No bibliography entry
 was added.
\item \emph{Duplicates with Parts~III, IV and~VI.} Equation~\eqref{hc:eq:cat} is
 Part~IV's \eqref{mj:eq:catalan-bounds}; \cref{hc:lem:endpoints} is Part~IV's
 \cref{mj:lem:first} and \eqref{mj:eq:last-count}, with a more uniform form
 of \eqref{mj:eq:last-half}, and its first-entry count is also Part~VI's
 \eqref{pr:eq:firstentry}; \eqref{hc:eq:masses} is Part~IV's
 \eqref{mj:eq:W}--\eqref{mj:eq:word-counts}; the two-piece weight and mass
 are Part~IV's \eqref{mj:eq:acceptance} and \eqref{mj:eq:wordmass}, and its
 normalized law is \eqref{mj:eq:word-law}, whose cost marginal is
 \eqref{mj:eq:cost-law}; \cref{hc:lem:small} is a second route to Part~VI's
 \cref{pr:lem:local,pr:lem:small}. They are kept, with merge notes, because
 Part~VII's limits are built on these exact forms. \Cref{hc:lem:parser}
 generalizes Part~IV's \cref{mj:lem:parse} to all $m<n$. New are
 \cref{hc:thm:window,hc:thm:phase}, the three-piece table \eqref{hc:eq:chi2}
 and its mass \eqref{hc:eq:8}, the corner and many-letter exclusions of
 \cref{hc:sec:remainders}, and the evaluation $I_3(1/2)=12\pi$; the
 manuscript's own list of new content (\cref{hc:sec:boundary}) agrees.
\item \emph{What Part~VII sharpens.} \Cref{hc:thm:window} turns Part~VI's
 \cref{pr:cor:half} into a limit and \cref{hc:thm:phase} turns
 \cref{pr:thm:staircase} into an equivalent for $1/3<\theta\le1/2$. Nothing
 in Parts~I--VI is refuted.
\item \emph{An unjustified step.} The bound $4(1/2)^j$ after
 \cref{hc:lem:small} is printed as delivered, followed by the editorial
 \cref{hc:rem:jbound}, which gives the missing justification and names the
 false reading. No other step of the manuscript was changed.
\item \emph{Notation.} Every rename is listed in \cref{hc:tab:notation}:
 $W,W_R,W_A\to\mathcal W(1/4),\mathcal W_R(1/4),\mathcal W_A(1/4)$ and
 $S,T\to S_n,T_n$; the manuscript's $\mathbb P$, positive part and cost
 are Part~III's $\ljP$, Part~VI's $\prpos{\cdot}$ and Part~III's
 $\ljcost$, and its thin space before each differential is written out. No
 normalization changed.
\item \emph{Labels.} The manuscript's 25 labels are kept with the prefix
 \texttt{hc:}; unprefixed, its generic \texttt{eq:8}, \texttt{eq:J},
 \texttt{eq:L} and \texttt{sec:remainders} would name nothing specific in a
 report of this size. The merge added labels to the new sections,
 subsections, tables and the editorial remark.
\item \emph{Research questions.} The manuscript's one paragraph of further
 questions is printed verbatim and then split into four subsections, each
 pointing to the question of Part~VI it continues.
\item \emph{Stale sentences elsewhere.} The abstract gained a paragraph;
 dated pointers to Part~VII were added where Parts~II, IV, V and~VI say
 that an equivalent below half size, the half-size transition function, the
 endpoint constant at $q=2$ or the amplitude at $r=2$ is open, to the
 caption of \cref{pr:tab:numerics}, and to Part~I's
 \cref{app:reproduction}; their original wording is kept.

\section{Further research questions}
\label{hc:sec:research}
The next concrete targets are exact constants in the lower phases, the
analogous crossover functions at $1/q$ for $q\ge3$, quantitative errors in
\cref{hc:thm:window}, and the conditioned distribution of the three
macroscopic pieces. The same parser suggests finite endpoint-state
calculations for those problems, but this manuscript claims only the results
stated above.

The four subsections below split that paragraph; the pointers in
parentheses are the merge's.

\subsection{Exact constants in the lower phases}
\label{hc:sec:q-lower}
Determine $\Omega_r(\theta)$ for $r\ge3$, $1/(r+1)<\theta<1/r$, and the
endpoint constants $\mathfrak K_q$ for $q\ge3$. (Part~VI's
\cref{pr:sec:q-amplitudes,pr:sec:q-endpoints}, now answered at $r=2$ and
$q=2$; for $r\ge3$ the parser has $r$ macroscopic outer letters and the
endpoint table of \eqref{hc:eq:chi2} has to be extended.)
[Added 1 October 2026, batch 72: answered in Part~VIII:
$\Omega_r(\theta)=\varkappa_{r+1}I_{r+1}(\theta)$ for every $r\ge3$
(\cref{gc:thm:phase}) and $\mathfrak K_q=\varkappa_{q+1}I_{q+1}(1/q)$ for
every $q\ge3$ (\cref{gc:thm:window}). The endpoint table extends as
proposed: the accepted channels are the low endpoint with no append and the
high endpoint with an initial prefix of append-containing gaps, of total mass
$2^{p-2}(p+1)$ (\cref{gc:sec:prefix,gc:rem:masses}).]

\subsection{Crossover functions at the higher reciprocal thresholds}
\label{hc:sec:q-higher}
Find the analogue of \cref{hc:thm:window} at $1/q$ for $q\ge3$, on Part~VI's
scale $\mathfrak w_q(n)$. (Part~VI's \cref{pr:sec:q-crossover}; at $q=2$ its
candidate $\mathfrak K_q+\mathfrak L_qt_+^{q-1}$ is now a theorem.)
[Added 1 October 2026, batch 72: answered for every $q\ge3$ in Part~VIII
(\cref{gc:thm:window}); the candidate holds with $\mathfrak L_q$ of
\eqref{gc:eq:A}.]

\subsection{Quantitative errors in the half-size window}
\label{hc:sec:q-errors}
Give a rate in \cref{hc:thm:window}. \Cref{hc:tab:finite} and
\cref{hc:sec:finite} show slow convergence, especially on the negative side
of the window, where the constant comes from an integrable corner. (Part~VI's
\cref{pr:sec:q-uniform} asks for uniform relative errors near every
reciprocal boundary.)
[Added 1 October 2026, batch 72: still open, and Part~VIII's limits for
$q\ge3$ have no rate either (\cref{gc:sec:q-rate}).]

\subsection{The conditioned three-piece law}
\label{hc:sec:q-geometry}
Find the limiting law of the three macroscopic pieces of a uniform avoider
conditioned on $M_n\le\lfloor\theta n\rfloor$, $1/3<\theta\le1/2$. The
endpoint table \eqref{hc:eq:chi2} and the density $(xyz)^{-3/2}$ of
\eqref{hc:eq:tripleweight} identify the leading families of the parser, but
no conditioned limit theorem is proved. (Part~VI's
\cref{pr:sec:q-geometry}; Part~IV's \cref{mj:thm:conditional} is the
two-piece analogue above half size.)
[Added 1 October 2026, batch 72: answered in Part~VIII, in every phase
(\cref{gc:cor:geometry}): conditionally on $M_n\le\lfloor\theta n\rfloor$,
$1/3<\theta\le1/2$, the parser's three macroscopic pieces converge to the
density $I_3(\theta)^{-1}(x_1x_2x_3)^{-3/2}$ on $\mathcal D_3(\theta)$,
independently of four uniform endpoint/gap channels.]


%% ====================================================================
%% Part VIII (batch 72): manuscript 41 of batch 72, "Exact Phase
%% Amplitudes and Reciprocal Crossovers for 132-Avoiders". All labels of
%% Part VIII carry the prefix gc: (general crossovers).
%% ====================================================================
\clearpage
\phantomsection\part*{Part VIII.\quad Every fixed phase amplitude and reciprocal crossover}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Part VIII.\texorpdfstring{\quad}{ } Every fixed phase amplitude and reciprocal crossover}

\noindent\emph{Part~VIII was added on 1 October 2026 in batch~72 of
ProveIt's incoming-report intake, from one manuscript. Part~VII proved the
first sub-half-size equivalent and the half-size crossover
(\cref{hc:thm:phase,hc:thm:window}) and asked for the higher constants, the
higher crossover functions and the conditioned piece law
(\cref{hc:sec:q-lower,hc:sec:q-higher,hc:sec:q-geometry}). Part~VIII proves
them for every fixed index: in every phase $1/p<\theta<1/(p-1)$,
\[
 \ljP\bigl(M_n\le\lfloor\theta n\rfloor\bigr)\sim
 \varkappa_p\,I_p(\theta)\,n^{-(p-1)/2},\qquad
 \varkappa_p=\frac{p+1}{2^p\pi^{(p-1)/2}},
\]
with a capped-simplex integral $I_p$, and at every reciprocal threshold
$1/r$, on Part~VI's scale $\mathfrak w_r(n)$,
\[
 n^{r/2}\,\ljP\bigl(M_n\le\lfloor n/r+t\,\mathfrak w_r(n)\rfloor\bigr)
 \longrightarrow\mathfrak K_r+\mathfrak L_r\,t_+^{r-1},
\]
locally uniformly in $t$; at $1/3$ the limit is
$5/\sqrt{2\pi}+(729\sqrt3/(4\pi))t_+^2$. Part~VII's two theorems are the cases
$p=3$ and $r=2$, and Part~IV's profile is the case $p=2$; they are printed
once, above, and cited here as the first cases of the general theorems. The
same proof gives the conditioned law of the parser's macroscopic pieces. This
answers Part~VII's questions on lower constants, higher crossovers and the
conditioned three-piece law, Part~VI's questions~74.1--74.3 and, at the level
of the parser's pieces, 74.4, and the amplitude half of Part~IV's
question~47.1.}

\section{Provenance, conventions and the answer}
\label{gc:sec:intro}

\subsection{One manuscript, one addition}
\label{gc:sec:source}
Part~VIII is built from one AI-assisted research manuscript,
\emph{manuscript~41} of batch~72, \emph{Exact Phase Amplitudes and Reciprocal
Crossovers for $132$-Avoiders}, dated 1 October 2026 (8 pages, A4, as
delivered; its byline reads ``Research note prepared for Vladimir Reshetnikov
with OpenAI''). It arrived as the archive
\texttt{General\_132\_Avoider\_Crossovers\_Package.zip} (inner directory
\texttt{General\_132\_Avoider\_Crossovers/}, main file
\texttt{general\_avoider\_crossovers.tex}) in ProveIt commit
\texttt{1512ef835}, and was placed in commit \texttt{9e6b04645}. Its source
ledger pins ProveIt commit \texttt{7421a4ca6}, at which this report's
\texttt{article.tex} had the blob \texttt{b9697af6c8} and contained
Parts~I--VII exactly as printed here; the report did not change between the
pin and the placement. So every statement of the manuscript about Parts~III,
IV, VI and~VII refers to the text printed above.

Its code, data, source ledger and checks README are shipped beside the earlier
parts' files with the prefix \path{09-general-crossovers-}, continuing the
report's local sequence after Part~VII's \path{08-half-size-crossover-}. Its
manuscript, README and PDF are not shipped and survive in the arrival commit;
its two checksum ledgers were verified at placement (15 of 15 and 8 of 8
files) and retired. Its \path{model.py} is byte-identical to Part~VI's
\path{code/07-polynomial-rarity-model.py} (Git blob \texttt{a4fdee3761}), which
the manuscript copied and credits; it is not shipped a third time, and the
rerun recipe of the report's README restores it under its delivered name
(\cref{gc:sec:reproduce}).

The manuscript contributes, in the order printed here: the equivalent in every
fixed phase (\cref{gc:thm:phase}) and the crossover limit at every fixed
reciprocal threshold (\cref{gc:thm:window}); uniform word bounds with a fixed
gap count and all moment orders (\cref{gc:lem:words}); counting bounds for
families with many pieces or a microscopic terminal child; the endpoint-prefix
count, whose accepted mass $2^{p-2}(p+1)$ generalizes the masses $3$ and $8$ of
Parts~IV and~VII (\cref{gc:sec:prefix}); the shrinking near-cap simplex at a
reciprocal threshold and its slope $\mathfrak L_r$; the background family, its
integrable corners and the microscopic terminal children; the evaluation
$I_4(1/3)=8\sqrt2\,\pi$ and the one-third window (\cref{gc:cor:third}); the
conditioned piece and channel law (\cref{gc:cor:geometry}) and the window
mixture (\cref{gc:cor:mixture}); finite regression checks; and its scope.
Every result, proof, remark, limitation and question of the manuscript is
printed. The Catalan, endpoint, parser and word-mass interfaces that it recalls
from Part~VII (and Parts~IV and~VI) are printed once, above, and cited by
label (\cref{gc:sec:interfaces}).

\begin{resultbox}
\textbf{Status (manuscript 41 of batch 72).} The theorems of Part~VIII have
written mathematical proofs. They are ordinary mathematical arguments, not
proof-assistant formalizations: nothing is Lean- or Rocq-verified, nothing has
been externally refereed, and no worldwide priority is claimed. The indices
$p$ and $r$ are fixed, and all constants and estimates may depend on them; no
uniform assertion as the index grows, no effective convergence rate and no
bridge to the fixed-bound spectral regime is made. The finite data are
regression checks, not evidence replacing any proof step; at $n\le192$ they are
still far from the one-third limit (\cref{gc:sec:finite}).
\end{resultbox}

\subsection{The questions of Parts~IV, VI and~VII, and what answers them}
\label{gc:sec:answer}
With $p_n(m)=\ljP(M_n\le m)$ as in \eqref{pr:eq:prob}, Part~VIII answers the
following questions.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Amplitudes in the lower phases} (Part~VI's
 \cref{pr:sec:q-amplitudes}, Part~VII's \cref{hc:sec:q-lower}, the amplitude
 half of Part~IV's \cref{mj:sec:q-hierarchy}). For every $r\ge1$ and
 $1/(r+1)<\theta<1/r$, \eqref{pr:eq:amplitudequestion} holds with
 \[
  \Omega_r(\theta)=\varkappa_{r+1}I_{r+1}(\theta)
 \]
 (\cref{gc:thm:phase} with $p=r+1$). The index shifts by one: Part~VIII's
 phase index $p$ counts macroscopic pieces, and Part~VI's $r=p-1$ is the
 exponent index. The case $r=1$ is Part~IV's \eqref{mj:eq:bulk-ratio}, the
 case $r=2$ Part~VII's \cref{hc:thm:phase}; $r\ge3$ is new. Part~IV's
 manuscript~11 asked for an equivalent for every fixed $0<\theta<1/2$; with
 the upper endpoints of the phases (\cref{gc:thm:phase}) one now exists for
 every fixed $0<\theta\le1/2$.
\item \emph{Constants at the reciprocal endpoints} (\cref{pr:sec:q-endpoints},
 \cref{hc:sec:q-lower}). For every $q\ge2$,
 $\mathfrak K_q=\lim_nn^{q/2}p_n(\lfloor n/q\rfloor)$ exists along all $n$ and
 equals $\varkappa_{q+1}I_{q+1}(1/q)$ (\cref{gc:thm:window} at $t=0$, and the
 endpoint clause of \cref{gc:thm:phase}); there is no residue-class
 oscillation in the limit. $\mathfrak K_2=6$ is Part~VII's;
 $\mathfrak K_3=5/\sqrt{2\pi}$ (\cref{gc:cor:third}); for $q\ge4$ the
 constant is a $q$-dimensional integral, not evaluated in closed form.
\item \emph{Limiting crossover functions} (\cref{pr:sec:q-crossover},
 \cref{hc:sec:q-higher}). For every $q\ge2$, \eqref{pr:eq:Hquestion} holds with
 Part~VI's candidate
 \[
  \mathfrak H_q(t)=\mathfrak K_q+\mathfrak L_qt_+^{q-1},\qquad
  \mathfrak L_q=\frac{(q+1)q^{5q/2-1}}{2^q\pi^{(q-1)/2}(q-1)!}.
 \]
 Part~VI called it ``a proposed hypothesis'' that ``could require a more
 complicated formula''; it is the theorem. At $q=2$ it is Part~VII's
 $6+(12/\sqrt\pi)t_+$.
\item \emph{The conditioned geometry} (\cref{pr:sec:q-geometry},
 \cref{hc:sec:q-geometry}). Conditionally on $M_n\le\lfloor\theta n\rfloor$ in
 a fixed phase, the ordered macroscopic piece vector of the canonical parser
 converges to the density $I_p(\theta)^{-1}\prod x_i^{-3/2}$ on
 $\mathcal D_p(\theta)$, independently of an endpoint/gap channel uniform over
 $p+1$ values, with independent gap words (\cref{gc:cor:geometry}); in a
 reciprocal window the law is a two-family mixture with uniform-simplex
 deficits (\cref{gc:cor:mixture}). This answers Part~VII's question as posed
 and Part~VI's question \emph{partly}: the conditioned permutation has exactly
 $p$ macroscopic parser pieces with probability tending to one, but their
 positions in the permutation, their value sets beyond the parser's ordering
 and the location of the largest jump are not described.
\item \emph{Transitions at every $\theta=1/r$} (Part~IV's
 \cref{cjm:sec:mj-half}, Part~V's \cref{pr:sec:cjm-half}). Every reciprocal
 threshold carries a window in the strong sense, a rescaled limit that is a
 nonconstant function of the scaled parameter, constant on the lower side.
\end{itemize}
The theorems sharpen Part~VI's \cref{pr:thm:staircase,pr:thm:cross} from
orders to equivalents and limits for every fixed index; nothing in
Parts~I--VII is refuted. Part~VIII does not answer Part~VII's question on
quantitative errors (\cref{hc:sec:q-errors}), or Part~VI's questions on
uniform relative errors, a growing reciprocal index, effective bounds, other
classes, other tail indices or formalization
(\cref{pr:sec:q-uniform,pr:sec:q-growing,pr:sec:q-effective,pr:sec:q-classes,pr:sec:q-tails,pr:sec:q-formal}).

\subsection{Notation of Part~VIII}
\label{gc:sec:notation}
Part~VI's $C_n$, $\Cat$, $\Av_n(132)$, $M_n$, $p_n(m)$, $\mathfrak w_q$,
$\mathfrak K_q$, $\mathfrak L_q$, $\mathfrak H_q$ and $\Omega_r$, Part~IV's
fixed fraction $\theta$, and Part~VII's $c_j=C_j/4^j$, endpoint letters $F$,
$L$, $J=N-F$, operations $R_\beta$ and $A$, word series $\mathcal W$,
$\mathcal W_R$, $\mathcal W_A$, small-word series $S_n$, $T_n$, constants $K$,
$K_B$, cost cutoff $H$, bound $B$ on $|t|$, cutoff $\varepsilon$ and tilt
constant $L$ keep their meanings. Part~VIII fixes the remaining names as follows
(\cref{gc:tab:notation}).
\begin{itemize}
\item The manuscript's endpoint factor $C_p^\star$ is printed $\varkappa_p$; a
 bare $C$ with an index is a Catalan number. The manuscript's window constants
 $B_r$, $A_r$ are printed with Part~VI's names $\mathfrak K_r$,
 $\mathfrak L_r$, and the limit function is $\mathfrak H_r$: they are not
 Part~I's $B_d$, Part~VII's bound $B$, the append operation $A$ or the
 generating function $A_m$. The manuscript's integration domain $D_p(\theta)$
 is printed $\mathcal D_p(\theta)$; it is not Part~III's limit variable $D$,
 the deficit $D_n$, or Part~V's compact set $\mathcal D$ of exponents.
\item The manuscript's coefficient $t_s=[z^s]T(z)^g$ is printed
 $T^{(g)}_s:=[z^s]T_n(z)^g$, because $t$ is the window variable. Its $S$, $T$
 are Part~VII's $S_n$, $T_n$.
\item The phase index $p$ is a number of macroscopic pieces, not Part~IV's or
 Part~V's moment order $p$; Part~VI's amplitude index is $r=p-1$, and its
 threshold index $q$ is the window index $r$ of \cref{gc:thm:window}.
\item The letter $\ell$ is a number of genuine macroscopic pieces in
 \eqref{gc:eq:many}--\eqref{gc:eq:manysmall} and a moment order in
 \eqref{gc:eq:moments}; it is not Part~VII's value of the last entry.
\item In \cref{gc:sec:constants} the manuscript's pair convolution $F_a(s)$ and
 auxiliary integral $J(A)$ are printed $\mathcal P_a(s)$ and
 $\mathcal J(\gamma)$, to keep them apart from the first entry $F$, the first
 deficiency $J$ and the operation $A$.
\end{itemize}
